# Audit of the whole game, October 2026 (#298)

Written by `npm run balance -- --audit --games 30 --months 24`; the sensitivity section is played only without `--no-sensitivity`. Every game is deterministic: the same command writes the same file. Everything below the hand-written line is kept.

## How the games play

- **Sensible player** (definitions 1–3, the sensitivity): the starting prices, $30 of marketing a day, open 6 days a week, the owner paid $700 a week; each morning, croissants for the last open day's demand (sold and asked for after they sold out) × 1.15, rounded up; a grocer's offer is accepted only when it is the careful chain's next one with its reserve on hand (#166), and no second one before month 2; renewals accepted; the emergency loan and Max Duvent accepted. Every event card of the content comes up, Mme Prudhomme's letters included, and the others are answered at random (definition 2: by their first choice), drawn from the seed and the day.
- **Best strategy** (definition 3, the sensitivity): #166's growth winner at each difficulty, played as `--growth` plays it, with Mme Prudhomme's letters added: Facile contracts three, bakery month4, no employee, dream, financing refuse; Moyen contracts three, bakery never, no employee, dream, financing refuse; Difficile contracts three, bakery never, no employee, dream, financing refuse.
- **Naive and careful players** (definition 5): #166's, played as `--growth` plays them, with the letters.
- **Careless player** (definition 7): prices 40 % above the start, 110 croissants a day, $80 of marketing a day, the owner paid $1,000 a week, the financing accepted; its games go bankrupt, so the letters' check has games to read.
- **Lever check** (definitions 4 and 6): each setting of a lever plays on from the same morning to the end of month 24, so the settings differ by that lever alone. Situations: calm, day 153 (October 1 of year 1), every grocer refused until then; summer, day 396 (June 1 of year 2), with the careful contracts; crunch, the morning after the naive player's first close below zero, every grocer signed on the first day one may, or after its lowest close of the first 6 months when it never goes below zero; affordable, for a rung of « Le rêve », the first morning the best strategy, buying nothing, has the cash for it and the rungs before it, at least three months before the end. A lever's setup (a bakery for the baker, a contract for the delivery person, the rungs before a rung) is decided in every setting alike. A lever matters when the gap between its settings' median net worth reaches 2 % of the baseline's median (or of $10,000.00 when that is smaller) in some situation. A game counts for a setting when it is strictly best there; tied best settings count for none, and a game where every setting ties is not counted; a setting dominates above 80 % of the counted games, pooled over situations and seeds, at each difficulty.
- **Sensitivity**: each numeric leaf of the balance files is moved on its own by -20 % and +20 %: whole numbers round half up, or move by one when that rounds back; probabilities stay within 0 and 1; a share of a whole (segment shares, an outcome's chances) moves and the others of its whole rescale to keep the sum; a value the game's loaders refuse is reported, not played. A leaf of one difficulty plays at that difficulty only, and a leaf of an event card the best strategy never sees plays the sensible player only. A tunable is dead when no game, of either policy, ended otherwise; a cliff moves survival by 10 points or more.
- 30 seeds (1 to 30) per difficulty, 24 months per game.

<!-- Written by hand: npm run balance keeps everything below this line. -->

## Findings

One line per problem, from the tables above (30 seeds, 24 months). Net worth is the median at the end of month 24; a percentage is against the setting the sensible player plays.

1. **The start is fair, and generous.** The sensible player survives 2 years in 30 of 30 games at every difficulty, with one emergency loan in 90 games (Difficile), and none goes bankrupt with every card on its first choice. The difficulties differ in wealth only: $169,242 at Facile, $81,191 at Moyen, $45,389 at Difficile. Definition 1's floors (95 / 85 / 65 %) leave room for a harder Difficile, but the definitions are floors, so nothing here tunes toward harder.
2. **The grocers' contracts are worth more than everything else together.** From a calm October with no contract, signing all three at once ends at $292,208 at Moyen against $81,191 for the careful chain (+260 %), +125 % at Facile and +347 % at Difficile; from June of year 2, +152 % at Moyen. #166's best strategy (all three on day 14) ends at $352,906 at Moyen. The trap still bites (the naive player is below zero in month 2 or 3 in 30 of 30 games at Moyen and Difficile), but one emergency loan carries every naive game through, so the trap costs a few weeks rather than the game.
3. **Max Duvent's shares sink net worth.** In the cash crunch, the loan alone ends at $338,251 at Moyen against $209,924 when his shares are sold too (−38 %); $297,566 against $179,559 at Difficile. Every setting that keeps him away wins the crunch: the owner paid $400 a week ($329,613 at Moyen), 70 croissants a day ($298,445 at Difficile), open 7 days ($289,480 at Difficile), no marketing ($226,499 at Difficile). #166 wanted his money worth taking in 10 to 50 % of the games that take it.
4. **Employees never pay** (#255, still true). An employee at the truck ends below nobody for every candidate in every situation: −48 % to −66 % from a calm October at Moyen (Maude: 18 of 30 games bankrupt), −19 % to −38 % from the summer, −10 % to −15 % from the crunch. Paying the minimum, so they quit after 8 weeks, beats their asking wage in 99 % of games, and letting them go after 13 weeks beats keeping them in 99 %. The delivery person (with one contract) and the baker (with the bakery open) never pay either.
5. **The bakery does not pay within 2 years.** The owner baking, oven leased, from a calm October: $43,971 at Moyen (10 of 30 bankrupt) against $81,191 without, $7,992 at Difficile (18 of 30 bankrupt). Only from the crunch, with three contracts to bake for, is it about even (+3 % at Facile).
6. **One price dominates.** Café at $3.00 (start $3.25) is strictly best in 99 to 100 % of games, and the croissant at $3.50 (start $3.75) in 98 to 100 %, in every situation and at every difficulty: the starting prices sit above the best, and no situation moves it. Chocolat's best is $2.50, below the $3.00 floor of definition 6.
7. **The sensible restock overbuys.** A fixed 70 croissants a day beats yesterday's demand + 15 % in 100 % of games ($84,377 against $81,191 at Moyen): a croissant keeps one day, so the margin is waste.
8. **Levers that move nothing.** The café and chocolat top-up rules (½× to 2×: under 2 % everywhere); trips to the Caisse, the fournil and the meunerie, once or weekly (at most 1.1 %, and the meunerie loses 3.8 % at Moyen for the hours away); the owner's pay outside a crunch (net worth counts the owner's cash and the company's alike, so pay moves money from one to the other); the financing outside a crunch (it never comes).
9. **Upgrades out of line over 2 years.** Personal rungs count in net worth at their price, so the vélo and the used car change nothing, and the condo, the luxury car, the house and the chalet are never affordable within 2 years on the best path. The espresso machine (+0.9 % at Moyen) and the bigger truck (+0.4 % at Moyen, +9 % at Difficile) barely pay; the second truck pays (+10 % at Moyen, +12 % at Facile).
10. **Winter storage on the Plateau** loses 62 % at Moyen, where walk-ins do not drop in winter: it is for Vieux-Montréal (#266).
11. **Lemonade** (#261): 20 a day in summer pays +2.8 % at Moyen; its price moves net worth by 3 %.
12. **No cliff, and survival never moves.** No tunable moved by ±20 % changes the survival of either policy by a single game at any difficulty: the game is far from its edge for these players. What moves net worth most is demand and the contracts: `grocers.json maxContracts` 3 → 2 costs the best strategy 39 to 44 %, Marché Bio-Logique's price ±20 % moves it ±12.6 % at Facile, and the habitués' reference prices of café and croissant, the Plateau's weekday crowd and the croissant's cost move the sensible player by 10 to 35 %, most at Difficile, where the margin is thinnest.
13. **One event card's weight swings Moyen by a fifth.** Removing « Cônes orange », « La machine tousse » or « La critique » from the draw (weight 1 → 0) raises the sensible player's median by 20 to 24 % at Moyen, though each costs far less on its own: changing one weight redraws the whole event stream, so the median at 30 seeds carries that noise. Read event-card rows of the tornado with this in mind.
14. **345 tunables are dead under these two policies**: the Vieux-Montréal district, lemonade's season and price for a player who sells none, the starting decisions both policies change, the employees' traits and wages for players who hire nobody, and event cards and choices that never came up or whose answer was never drawn. They are inactive for these players, not unused by the engine; the lever check covers the decisions behind most of them.
